Foreword THE GENESIS

The Story of Two Engineers and Late-Night Ambition

In mid-2026, technical interviewing had become an arms race. Companies were subjecting senior engineers, new graduates, and career switchers to grueling 5-round gauntlets: complex LeetCode hard problems, distributed system design under time pressure, and multi-threaded concurrency traps.

Several tools attempted to address this: Cluely AI, Parakeet AI, Interview Fox, and Coprep AI. Yet, every single one of them suffered from three fatal flaws that rendered them hazardous or unusable in high-stakes interviews:

  • The Screen-Share Trap: Most tools relied on basic OS browser windows or crude desktop wrappers. The moment a candidate shared their screen on Zoom, Microsoft Teams, or Google Meet, or ran proctoring software like HackerRank Proctored, the copilot window would be instantly visible to the interviewer, leading to immediate disqualification.
  • The 3-Second Latency Chasm: Standard LLM wrappers routed requests through slow HTTP intermediaries or heavy reasoning chains. By the time an answer streamed back (3 to 5 seconds later), the conversation had moved on, and awkward silence destroyed candidate credibility.
  • The Privacy Breach: Existing platforms forced candidates to upload their full PDF resumes and personal identities to third-party cloud servers without encryption, storing candidate profiles on untrusted databases.

Roushan Kumar and Ayushi Raj refused to accept these compromises. Working late into the night across months of relentless experimentation, they formulated the core thesis of Coco AI:

🔮 The Coco AI Core Invariant
"A true copilot must be completely undetectable to the operating system's window compositors, must deliver streaming tokens in under 200 milliseconds, and must speak in the exact voice, projects, and personal stack of the candidate."

Chapter 1: System Architecture CH 01

The Tripartite Topology

Coco AI is engineered not as a monolithic desktop executable, but as an interconnected trinity of systems:

The Sub-200ms Latency Rule

👨‍💻 Dad's Engineering Note (Roushan)
"When you are sitting in a technical interview with Google or Uber, every 100 milliseconds feels like an eternity. If the interviewer finishes asking 'How does Redis handle cluster rebalancing?' and your copilot takes 3 seconds to respond, you can't use it. That is why Ayushi and I banned heavy middleware servers. By opening direct TLS 1.3 socket streams from your local machine to Cerebras LPUs, tokens start printing before you've even cleared your throat."

In live conversational psychology, human speech pauses between turns average 200 to 400 milliseconds. If an assistant begins streaming after 800ms, the candidate appears hesitant. If it streams at 1,500ms, the candidate has already stalled.

To achieve sub-200ms Time-To-First-Token (TTFT), Coco AI bypasses all intermediary cloud gateways:

  1. No Middleman Gateway: Requests are dispatched directly from the client machine to edge inference clusters (Cerebras LPU and Groq) using direct keep-alive HTTPS/SSE sockets.
  2. Zero Pre-flight Overhead: Pre-warmed HTTP agents and raw TLS 1.3 session caches eliminate handshake penalties.
  3. Cerebras LPU Architecture: Cerebras Wafer-Scale Engines achieve speeds exceeding 2,000 tokens/second on gpt-oss-120b, allowing complete algorithmic blocks to materialize before the interviewer has even looked away.
🧪 Junior Architect Lab 01: Benchmarking Time-to-First-Token 15-Min Challenge

Goal: Write a mini Node.js script using performance.now() to measure the real-world latency difference between a standard cloud API and Cerebras LPU. 

// lab01_ttft.js
const https = require('https');
const start = performance.now();

const req = https.request({
  hostname: 'api.cerebras.ai',
  path: '/v1/chat/completions',
  method: 'POST',
  headers: {
    'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + process.env.CEREBRAS_API_KEY,
    'Content-Type': 'application/json'
  }
}, (res) => {
  res.once('data', () => {
    const ttft = (performance.now() - start).toFixed(1);
    console.log(`⚡ Time to First Token (TTFT): ${ttft} ms`);
  });
});
req.write(JSON.stringify({
  model: 'gpt-oss-120b',
  messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'Say hello' }],
  stream: true
}));
req.end();

Expected Result: You should observe a TTFT between 150ms and 220ms!

Chapter 2: Project Anatomy CH 02

The Clean Separation Principle

A common mistake in Electron development is mixing UI code with node-level execution. Coco AI enforces strict context isolation, splitting responsibilities cleanly:

Directory / File Runtime Scope Primary Responsibility
main.js Node.js (Main) Window lifecycle, Win32 hardware stealth hook, global shortcuts, screen capture, cocoai:// deep-link protocol handler.
preload.js Isolated Bridge Secure IPC bridge exposing whitelist methods (captureScreen, installUpdateNow, onAuthSynced) to window.electronAPI.
app.js Chromium (Renderer) State controller, question submission, prompt synthesis, response markdown formatter, PDF parser, audio visualizer.
services/cerebras.js Node.js (Main/Service) High-speed LPU text completion streaming engine with 402/429 failover triggers.
services/groq.js Node.js (Main/Service) Zero-latency neural fallback streaming service.
services/gemini.js Renderer (Browser Fetch) Multimodal screen solver with gemini-3.8-flash dynamic failover chain.
services/nvidia.js Renderer (Browser Fetch) NVIDIA NIM secondary vision fallback engine (minimaxai/minimax-m3).
services/deepgram.js Renderer (Web Audio) WASAPI system loopback audio capture and real-time Nova-3 WebSocket client.
Publish_Release.bat Windows Batch 1-click execution wrapper that invokes PowerShell with execution bypass.
publish_release.ps1 PowerShell v3.0 Automated tag discovery, version bump, git commit/push, NSIS compilation, and GitHub REST release publisher.
website/Landing Page/ React 19 + Vite Cloud SaaS landing page, Supabase auth/user profiles, and Razorpay checkout engine.

Complete Repository Directory Hierarchy

To build Coco AI with zero architectural confusion, every file in the repository must live in its exact intended domain. Below is the comprehensive directory tree mapping every file, manifest, script, and database migration to its physical storage location:

NEVER commit to git) ├── .gitignore # Git ignore rules (filters node_modules, dist, .env) ├── AGENTS.md # Founder-enforced immutable engineering invariants ├── app.js # Renderer HUD controller & real-time UI logic ├── electron-builder.yml # NSIS Windows installer & auto-updater manifest ├── index.html # HUD DOM hierarchy & cosmic glassmorphism layout ├── main.js # Electron main process & Win32 hardware stealth ├── package.json # Desktop app dependencies, scripts & metadata ├── preload.js # Context-isolated security bridge (IPC whitelist) ├── Publish_Release.bat # 1-click batch launcher for production releases ├── publish_release.ps1 # Automated version bumping, git push & GitHub release └── style.css # Cosmic dark theme tokens & micro-animations

Step-by-Step Scaffolding Blueprint: Building from Day 1

If you are initializing Coco AI on a completely empty computer with zero files, this is the exact chronological sequence to construct the folder scaffolding:

  1. Initialize the Root Workspace: Create the root project directory and navigate into it: 
    mkdir "coco-ai"
cd "coco-ai"
npm init -y
    This creates the base package.json file at the root.
  2. Create the Specialized Subdirectories: Run the following terminal commands to scaffold each isolated module: 
    mkdir "services"
mkdir "assets"
mkdir -p "website/Landing Page"
mkdir -p ".agents/skills/coco-ai-expert"
  3. Populate the Core Application Files: Create the root execution files: 
    touch main.js preload.js index.html style.css app.js .env electron-builder.yml

Folder-by-Folder Architectural Blueprint & Naming Rules

Each folder in Coco AI has a strict naming convention, a specific lifecycle purpose, and rigorous storage rules. Understanding why each file lives where it does is essential to mastering this codebase:

1. The Root Directory (/) — The Electron Orchestration Layer

The root folder is the primary workspace for the Electron desktop client. Files stored here belong directly to the desktop application's lifecycle, the UI presentation layer, or root packaging scripts:

  • main.js (Node.js Main Process): Must reside at the root because it is registered as the application's entrypoint in package.json ("main": "main.js"). It controls the window lifecycle, hardware display protection (SetWindowDisplayAffinity), global hotkeys, and native screen capture.
  • preload.js (The Context-Isolated Security Bridge): Must be stored at root alongside main.js. In main.js, Electron references it via: 
    webPreferences: {
  preload: path.join(__dirname, 'preload.js'),
  contextIsolation: true,
  nodeIntegration: false
}
    Keeping preload.js at the root ensures that path.join(__dirname, 'preload.js') resolves seamlessly in both local development and inside the packaged ASAR archive.
  • index.html & style.css (HUD Presentation Layer): The visual shell. main.js loads index.html via mainWindow.loadFile('index.html'). index.html links to style.css via standard HTML relative linkage (<link rel="stylesheet" href="style.css">).
  • app.js (Renderer State Controller): The brain of the frontend HUD. Imported at the bottom of index.html (<script src="app.js"></script>). It drives question queueing, markdown rendering, audio waveform meters, and communicates with preload.js through window.electronAPI.
  • package.json & electron-builder.yml (Build Manifests): Define desktop runtime dependencies (electron, dotenv, electron-updater) and packaging instructions. electron-builder.yml specifies the NSIS installer target, custom icon paths, and the GitHub release repository for silent updates.
  • Publish_Release.bat & publish_release.ps1 (Automated Release Pipeline): Kept in the root directory so the engineer can trigger a worldwide deployment simply by double-clicking Publish_Release.bat in Windows File Explorer or running it from the root terminal.
  • .env (Environment Configuration): Must be stored in the root directory so that require('dotenv').config({ path: path.join(__dirname, '.env') }) in main.js locates your API keys instantly. Critical Rule: .env must be listed in .gitignore and NEVER committed to public version control.

2. The Services Directory (/services/) — The Isolated Micro-Drivers

Folder Naming Convention: Named exactly services (lowercase, plural). This folder houses isolated driver modules that interface with external APIs, audio hardware, or heavy offline libraries:

  • The Separation of Execution Scopes: Notice that the files inside services/ operate across two distinct runtimes:
    • Node.js Main Process Drivers (cerebras.js, groq.js): These require Node's native https module and raw socket control to achieve sub-200ms latency without browser preflight overhead. They are required directly by main.js via: 
      const cerebras = require('./services/cerebras');
const groq = require('./services/groq');
    • Chromium Renderer Drivers (gemini.js, nvidia.js, deepgram.js, pdf.min.js): These run inside the browser window context. They utilize browser Web APIs (Web Audio API for WASAPI loopback, Web Workers for PDF.js, and browser fetch() for vision). They are loaded into index.html via: 
      <script src="services/pdf.min.js"></script>
<script src="services/deepgram.js"></script>
<script src="services/gemini.js"></script>
<script src="services/nvidia.js"></script>
  • Why They Must Be in services/: Keeping all third-party AI and audio logic inside services/ ensures modularity. If Cerebras updates its model catalog, or Deepgram releases Nova-4, you only update a single file inside services/ without touching window stealth, IPC bridges, or UI styles.

3. The Assets Directory (/assets/) — Visual Identity & Binaries

Folder Naming Convention: Named assets. Contains graphic assets, brand logos, and operating system icon binaries:

  • coco_logo.ico (Windows Application Icon): A compiled Windows icon resource containing multiple embedded resolution frames (16x16, 32x32, 48x48, 64x64, 128x128, and 256x256 pixels). It is referenced by main.js: 
    icon: path.join(__dirname, 'assets', 'coco_logo.ico')
    and by electron-builder.yml during installer compilation.
  • icon.png (Transparent Brand Logo): A 512x512 transparent PNG used inside the desktop HUD header (<img src="assets/icon.png">) and on the public marketing landing page.

4. The Cloud Web Hub (/website/Landing Page/) — The Dual-Project Rule

Folder Naming Convention: website/Landing Page/. This folder contains the entire web presence, marketing website, user dashboard, and Supabase database migrations:

⚡ The Architectural Reason for Dual-Project Isolation
Why isn't the website merged into the root folder? Because the desktop client and the web application have fundamentally incompatible runtime environments:
  • The desktop app runs on Electron + Node.js (CommonJS) with native Win32 bindings.
  • The web platform runs on React 19 + Vite + TypeScript (ESM) deployed to edge servers (Vercel/Netlify).
By keeping the website inside website/Landing Page/ with its own independent package.json, web dependencies (like Vite, React, TanStack Router) never inflate the desktop binary, and desktop packaging tools (electron-builder) ignore the web folder completely via the exclusion rule: 
files:
  - "**/*"
  - "!website/**"
  - "!dist/**"

5. The Agent Customizations Directory (/.agents/) — AI Memory

Folder Naming Convention: .agents/ (prefixed with a dot so it remains discrete):

  • skills/coco-ai-expert/SKILL.md: Contains the persistent system memory, architectural invariants, active AI models catalog, and recovery runbooks for AI pair-programming agents (like Antigravity). If you ever open this repository on a fresh machine or new AI session, the agent reads this file to instantly regain full architectural mastery.
  • AGENTS.md (in root): The immutable engineering constitution enforcing rules: public repository visibility for silent auto-updates, perpetual hardware stealth protection, and founder whitelist access.

Cross-File Relative Storage & Reference Map

To see how every file connects across folders, consult this definitive reference table:

Calling File Target File Relative Storage Path Syntax Used in Code

Chapter 3: The Stealth Desktop Shell CH 03

The Physics of Screen-Share Invisibility

👩‍🎨 Mom's Design Note (Ayushi)
"The stealth guarantee isn't just about code — it's about human psychology. If an applicant is terrified that their screen share might flicker and reveal their copilot window, their confidence evaporates. We made sure that even if the candidate accidentally clicks 'Share Entire Desktop' on Zoom or HackerRank Proctored, Windows treats Coco AI as if it simply does not exist. That psychological safety transforms a nervous candidate into a confident engineer."

How does a window remain completely visible to the person sitting at the physical monitor, while being completely blacked out or excluded from Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Discord, and Google Meet?

The secret lies in the Windows Desktop Window Manager (DWM) compositor. Windows 10 (Build 2004+) introduced an internal window display attribute known as WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE (value 0x00000011).

🛡️ The Win32 Compositor Hook
When an application calls SetWindowDisplayAffinity(hwnd, WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE), Windows instructs DirectX and the Desktop Duplication API to completely ignore the window's visual tree during compositor capture passes. The user sees the window normally, but screen capture buffers receive the pixels directly beneath it!

In Electron, this capability is exposed through mainWindow.setContentProtection(true). Here is how Coco AI initializes this during window creation in main.js: 

function createWindow() {
  const primaryDisplay = screen.getPrimaryDisplay();
  const { width, height } = primaryDisplay.workAreaSize;

  mainWindow = new BrowserWindow({
    width: 880,
    height: 640,
    x: Math.round((width - 880) / 2),
    y: 40,
    frame: false,
    transparent: true,
    alwaysOnTop: true,
    skipTaskbar: true,
    resizable: true,
    hasShadow: false,
    webPreferences: {
      nodeIntegration: false,
      contextIsolation: true,
      preload: path.join(__dirname, 'preload.js'),
    },
  });

  // ─── CRITICAL HARDWARE STEALTH INVARIANT ────────────────────────
  // Guarantees 100% invisibility to Zoom, Teams, Meet, and proctoring tools.
  mainWindow.setContentProtection(true);

  // Set window levels above standard applications
  mainWindow.setAlwaysOnTop(true, 'screen-saver');
  mainWindow.setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces(true, { visibleOnFullScreen: true });
}

The Panic Mechanism & Emergency Shutdown

Under strict proctoring checks, candidates might face sudden screen audits where an interviewer says: "Please minimize all windows and show me your desktop taskbar."

Coco AI engineers two layers of defense into global OS hooks:

  • Panic Freeze (Ctrl + Shift + P): Instantly executes mainWindow.hide(), sets isOverlayVisible = false, and halts state updates without terminating background connections.
  • Emergency Kill (Ctrl + Shift + Q): Bypasses all confirmation dialogs and calls app.quit() to instantly purge Coco AI from OS memory in 0ms.
🧪 Junior Architect Lab 03: The OBS Studio Hardware Stealth Test 15-Min Challenge

Goal: Build a 25-line Electron application that proves hardware stealth against OBS Studio, Zoom, or Discord desktop sharing. 

// lab03_stealth.js
const { app, BrowserWindow } = require('electron');

app.whenReady().then(() => {
  const win = new BrowserWindow({
    width: 500, height: 300,
    transparent: true, frame: false, alwaysOnTop: true
  });
  // The magic Win32 DirectX flag:
  win.setContentProtection(true);
  win.loadURL('data:text/html,<h1 style="color:red;background:white;">TOP SECRET COCO AI</h1>');
});

Challenge: Open OBS Studio or Discord, start sharing your screen, and observe that while you can see the window, the screen recording shows 100% black transparency!

Chapter 4: Acoustic Intelligence CH 04

Capturing Pure System Loopback

👨‍💻 Dad's Engineering Note (Roushan)
"Never attempt to record the interviewer through your laptop microphone! The room acoustics, fan noise, and speaker echo will destroy Speech-to-Text accuracy. Capturing pure system loopback directly from the Windows soundcard means the interviewer's voice arrives in pristine 16kHz uncompressed PCM — exactly as it left their audio driver."

Traditional interview tools attempt to record the interviewer via the candidate's laptop microphone. This produces terrible results: room echoes, typing noise on mechanical keyboards, and the candidate's own breathing pollute the transcript.

Coco AI solves this using Windows WASAPI system loopback audio via Electron's desktopCapturer: 

// main.js: Provide system audio loopback source ID
ipcMain.handle('get-system-audio-source-id', async () => {
  const sources = await desktopCapturer.getSources({ types: ['screen'] });
  if (sources.length > 0) {
    return sources[0].id;
  }
  throw new Error('No audio loopback screen source found');
});

In services/deepgram.js, this stream is captured directly from the soundcard output and fed into Deepgram Nova-3 over a persistent WebSocket: 

const stream = await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({
  audio: {
    mandatory: {
      chromeMediaSource: 'desktop',
      chromeMediaSourceId: sourceId
    }
  },
  video: false
});

// Configure real-time WebSocket connection to Deepgram Nova-3
const socket = new WebSocket(
  'wss://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen?model=nova-3&smart_format=true&interim_results=true&utterance_end_ms=3000&endpointing=1500',
  ['token', apiKey]
);

The Conversational Debounce Algorithm

An interviewer rarely speaks in a single continuous burst. They say: "Can you explain how...", pause for 0.8 seconds to gather their thoughts, and continue: "...you would scale this database?"

If the AI triggers on the first pause, it begins generating an answer to an incomplete question. Coco AI engineers a dual-threshold debounce:

  • endpointing=1500: Deepgram waits for 1.5 seconds of sustained silence before finalizing a sentence segment.
  • utterance_end_ms=3000: A full 3-second utterance window ensures multi-sentence interview questions are synthesized as a single contextual prompt.
🧪 Junior Architect Lab 04: Terminal Acoustic RMS Decibel Meter 15-Min Challenge

Goal: Calculate the Root Mean Square (RMS) energy of an audio buffer and render a real-time terminal volume bar using Unicode blocks: ▂▃▄▅▆▇█. 

function calculateRMS(float32Array) {
  let sum = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < float32Array.length; i++) {
    sum += float32Array[i] * float32Array[i];
  }
  const rms = Math.sqrt(sum / float32Array.length);
  const blocks = [' ', ' ', '▂', '▃', '▄', '▅', '▆', '▇', '█'];
  const index = Math.min(blocks.length - 1, Math.floor(rms * 10 * blocks.length));
  process.stdout.write('\rAcoustic RMS: [' + blocks[index].repeat(15) + '] ' + rms.toFixed(4));
}

Chapter 5: The Multi-Engine AI Brain CH 05

The September 2026 Model Catalog

👩‍🎨 Mom's Design Note (Ayushi)
"We tested dozens of AI models during late-night mock interviews. Big frontier models often gave answers that were 400 words long — completely unnatural for a live human candidate to say! That's why we tuned our system prompts with strict word limits: 40–70 words for conceptual questions, and STAR methodology for behavioral queries. You must sound like an articulate human, not an encyclopedia."

AI model providers rapidly deprecate and retire models. A production interview copilot cannot rely on a single model or outdated identifiers. Coco AI implements an evergreen catalog: 

// services/cerebras.js: Active Cerebras LPU Models
const MODELS = {
  'gpt-120b':  'gpt-oss-120b',  // Default: Flagship reasoning & coding
  'qwen-27b':  'qwen-3.8-27b',  // High-efficiency competitive programming
  'gemma-31b': 'gemma-4-31b',   // Algorithmic analysis
};

// services/groq.js: Active Groq Models
const MODELS = {
  'gpt-120b':  'openai/gpt-oss-120b', // Default 0ms failover
  'qwen-27b':  'qwen/qwen3.8-27b',
  'gpt-20b':   'openai/gpt-oss-20b',   // Ultra-low latency mode
};

// services/gemini.js: Active Multimodal Vision Models
MODEL_CHAIN: [
  'gemini-3.8-flash',    // Primary: ~300ms vision latency, 200 OK
  'gemini-3.7-flash',    // High-capacity fallback
  'gemini-3.5-flash',    // Stable fallback
  'gemini-flash-latest', // Dynamic alias
]

The Multi-Screenshot Scroll Buffer (Ctrl + Shift + S)

Modern coding assessments (LeetCode, HackerRank, CodeSignal) feature problem descriptions that span 2 to 3 screen heights. Taking a single screenshot only captures the top half, missing constraints and sample test cases.

Coco AI engineers visual context fusion:

  1. The candidate presses Ctrl + Shift + S. Coco AI captures the top half and appends it to state.screenshotBuffer = [img1].
  2. The candidate scrolls down and presses Ctrl + Shift + S again. Coco AI appends state.screenshotBuffer = [img1, img2].
  3. The candidate presses Ctrl + Shift + A (or the final capture). Coco AI bundles all screenshots into a single multimodal payload to gemini-3.8-flash.
  4. The prompt explicitly instructs the neural network: "You are receiving N screenshots of the SAME problem. The user scrolled down to capture the full question. Combine ALL visible information from ALL images before writing your solution."

Chapter 6: The Cosmic Stealth HUD CH 06

The Cosmic Glassmorphism System

👩‍🎨 Mom's Design Note (Ayushi)
"Eye-tracking physics: When candidates read notes during an interview, their pupils dart across the screen, which interviewers immediately notice. We designed Ghost Click-Through mode and anchored the HUD 20 pixels directly beneath the webcam with 85% opacity. When you look at Coco AI's answers, your eyes are looking directly down the lens of the camera — giving the interviewer 100% natural eye contact."

Coco AI's visual identity was architected by Ayushi Raj, combining an obsidian black foundation with cosmic violet hues: 

/* style.css: Core Design Tokens */

The Three Adaptive Stealth Layouts

  • Full Dual-Pane (880px): Live transcript on the right, instantaneous AI answer cards on the left. Ideal for candidates with high-resolution screens.
  • Compact Overlay (520px): Condenses the UI to display only the active solution card with notification badges, taking up minimal screen real estate.
  • Ghost Click-Through: Sets window opacity to 15% and activates mainWindow.setIgnoreMouseEvents(true, { forward: true }). The candidate can click, type, and select code in their IDE directly through the Coco AI window without focusing it!

Chapter 7: Context Injection Engine CH 07

100% Client-Side PDF Extraction

👨‍💻 Dad's Engineering Note (Roushan)
"Your resume is your identity — your real name, phone number, work history, and proprietary project details. Competing tools force users to upload their PDFs to third-party databases where they can be scraped or sold. By bundling PDF.js locally into the client, your resume never leaves your computer. Only summarized contextual tokens are injected into your active session."

When an interviewer asks: "Tell me about a time you optimized a slow query," generic answers sound artificial. A winning answer references the candidate's actual projects, real metrics, and specific tech stack.

To ensure absolute candidate privacy, Coco AI extracts resumes entirely within the local browser thread using PDF.js: 

// app.js: In-browser local PDF parsing
async function parseResumePdf(file) {
  const arrayBuffer = await file.arrayBuffer();
  const pdf = await pdfjsLib.getDocument({ data: arrayBuffer }).promise;
  let fullText = '';

  for (let i = 1; i <= pdf.numPages; i++) {
    const page = await pdf.getPage(i);
    const content = await page.getTextContent();
    fullText += content.items.map(item => item.str).join(' ') + '\n';
  }

  // Stored strictly in local client memory
  localStorage.setItem('cocoai_resume_text', fullText);
  return fullText;
}

Chapter 8: The Modern Web SaaS Hub CH 08

The Tech Stack

The cloud landing page and account portal (deployed at https://coco-ai-cyan.vercel.app) was engineered using the state-of-the-art 2026 web stack:

  • React 19: Utilizing the React compiler and server-ready client hooks.
  • TanStack Router: Type-safe URL routing and nested layouts.
  • Tailwind CSS v4: Lightning-fast CSS-first compilation without legacy JavaScript configs.
  • Vite 8: Instant HMR and rollup production chunking.

The Dynamic Chunk Preload Recovery

In high-traffic Single Page Applications, deploying a new build invalidates old component chunk hashes. If a user scrolls while an update is deployed, their browser throws: Failed to fetch dynamically imported module: comparison-xxx.js.

Coco AI engineered a permanent self-healing listener in main.tsx: 

// website/Landing Page/src/main.tsx
window.addEventListener('vite:preloadError', (event) => {
  console.warn('[Vite] Chunk preload error detected. Reloading to get latest build assets...');
  event.preventDefault();
  window.location.reload();
});

Chapter 9: Database, Auth & Monetization CH 09

Supabase PostgreSQL Architecture

The entire user database is hosted on Supabase (Project: csntdpytzqcwceikdfyz) with Row Level Security (RLS) enforcing that users can only read and mutate their own profile: 

-- 001_user_profiles.sql: Schema Definition
create table if not exists public.user_profiles (
  id uuid references auth.users on delete cascade primary key,
  email text not null,
  display_name text,
  avatar_url text,
  subscription_tier text not null default 'free'
    check (subscription_tier in ('free', 'standard', 'pro', 'developer')),
  tokens_remaining bigint not null default 50000,
  tokens_limit bigint not null default 50000,
  audio_minutes_remaining double precision not null default 30.0,
  audio_minutes_limit double precision not null default 30.0,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now()
);

alter table public.user_profiles enable row level security;

create policy "Users can view own profile"
  on public.user_profiles for select
  using (auth.uid() = id);

The 1-Click Desktop Auth Protocol (cocoai://)

To connect their desktop client, users do not enter clunky API keys. They click "Open & Sync Desktop App" on the website, which invokes a registered Win32 protocol: 

// main.js: Register and handle cocoai:// deep-links
if (process.defaultApp) {

Chapter 10: 1-Click Global Auto-Updates CH 10

The Distribution Challenge

Distributing Electron binaries manually is exhausting: building the 85MB installer, sending download links, telling users to uninstall the old version, and losing users who never update.

Roushan and Ayushi built an automated 1-click publishing pipeline:

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ GLOBAL AUTO-UPDATE DELIVERY PIPELINE │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ 1. Developer double-clicks "Publish_Release.bat" │ ▼ 2. "publish_release.ps1" runs with ExecutionPolicy Bypass ├─► Bumps version in package.json (v1.0.45 ➔ v1.0.46) ├─► Creates Git Tag & Pushes to GitHub remote ├─► Runs "electron-builder --publish never" └─► Uploads .exe, .blockmap & latest.yml via GitHub REST API │ ▼ 3. GitHub Releases hosts binary on public repo "Hey-Astreon/COCO-AI" │ ▼ 4. Installed client runs "autoUpdater.checkForUpdates()" on launch ├─► Compares latest.yml against installed package.json ├─► Downloads differential binary patch in background └─► Shows green toast: "🆕 Coco AI v1.0.46 is ready to install!" │ ▼ 5. User clicks "Restart & Update" ➔ autoUpdater.quitAndInstall() App restarts on the latest version in 2 seconds flat!

Chapter 11: Battle-Hardening War Stories CH 11

The Infamous Indian ISP IPv6 Socket Drop

During beta testing, Indian users on Airtel and Jio Wi-Fi networks experienced mysterious 20-second stalls when contacting Google's Gemini API and Cerebras endpoints.

The Root Cause: Windows Wi-Fi drivers prioritize IPv6 DNS records (AAAA). The ISPs advertised IPv6 routes that stalled or dropped packets upstream. Node.js would wait for the 20-second TCP SYN timeout on the IPv6 address (2001:4860:...) before falling back to IPv4.

The Permanent Solution: We enforced IPv4-first resolution across both Node.js and Chromium network layers in main.js: 

// main.js: The Dual IPv4 Hardening Fix
// 1. Force Node.js https module (Cerebras & Groq) to prefer IPv4
require('dns').setDefaultResultOrder('ipv4first');

// 2. Force Chromium renderer fetch() (Gemini Vision) to disable IPv6
app.commandLine.appendSwitch('disable-ipv6');

Section B: Electron Main Process SEC B

main.js is the foundation of the desktop application. It initialises the transparent, frameless BrowserWindow, enforces Win32 hardware-level screen capture invisibility, binds global operating system hotkeys, captures display frames, and syncs deep-link authentication tokens.

main.js Node.js (Main Process) · 602 lines 
/* ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   CocoAI — Electron Main Process
   Multi-Layer Stealth System + AI/Audio IPC Bridge
   ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ */

// Load environment variables FIRST
require('dotenv').config({ path: require('path').join(__dirname, '.env') });

// ─── IPv4-First DNS Resolution (fixes IPv6 timeout on Indian ISPs) ──────────
// Forces Node.js https module (used by Cerebras/Groq) to prefer IPv4 addresses.
// Without this, connections to AI APIs can stall on IPv6 routes that time out.
require('dns').setDefaultResultOrder('ipv4first');

const { app, BrowserWindow, globalShortcut, ipcMain, screen, desktopCapturer, shell } = require('electron');
const path = require('path');

// ─── Auto-Updater (only in packaged builds) ──────────────────
let autoUpdater = null;
if (app.isPackaged) {
  try {
    autoUpdater = require('electron-updater').autoUpdater;
    autoUpdater.autoDownload = true;      // Download update in background
    autoUpdater.autoInstallOnAppQuit = true; // Install on next restart
    autoUpdater.logger = console;
  } catch (e) {
    console.warn('⚠️ electron-updater not available:', e.message);
  }
}
let cerebras = null;
try {
  cerebras = require('./services/cerebras');
} catch (e) {
  console.warn('⚠️ Cerebras service failed to load — text AI will be disabled.', e.message);
}

let groq = null;
try {
  groq = require('./services/groq');
  console.log('✅ Groq fallback engine loaded.');
} catch (e) {
  console.warn('⚠️ Groq service failed to load — fallback AI will be disabled.', e.message);
}

// ─── Custom Protocol Registration (cocoai://) ────────────────────
if (process.defaultApp) {
  if (process.argv.length >= 2) {
    app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('cocoai', process.execPath, [path.resolve(process.argv[1])]);
  }
} else {
  app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('cocoai');
}

function handleDeepLinkUrl(urlStr) {
  if (!urlStr || typeof urlStr !== 'string') return;
  try {
    if (urlStr.startsWith('cocoai://')) {
      const parsedUrl = new URL(urlStr);
      const sessionParam = parsedUrl.searchParams.get('session');
      if (sessionParam) {
        const decoded = JSON.parse(decodeURIComponent(sessionParam));
        if (mainWindow && mainWindow.webContents) {
          mainWindow.webContents.send('auth-session-synced', decoded);
        }
      }
    }
  } catch (e) {
    console.warn('Failed to parse deep link url:', e);
  }
}

// ─── Single Instance Lock ──────────────────────────────────────
const gotTheLock = app.requestSingleInstanceLock();
if (!gotTheLock) {
  app.quit();
} else {
  app.on('second-instance', (event, commandLine) => {
    const urlArg = Array.isArray(commandLine) ? commandLine.find(arg => arg && arg.startsWith('cocoai://')) : null;
    if (urlArg) {
      handleDeepLinkUrl(urlArg);
    }
    if (mainWindow) {
      if (!isOverlayVisible) {
        mainWindow.show();
        isOverlayVisible = true;
      }
      if (mainWindow.isMinimized()) mainWindow.restore();
      mainWindow.focus();
    }
  });
}

app.on('open-url', (event, urlStr) => {
  event.preventDefault();
  handleDeepLinkUrl(urlStr);
});

// ─── Stealth Layer 0: Process Title Disguise ────────────────────
app.setName('System Host Service');
if (process.platform === 'win32') {
  app.setAppUserModelId('Microsoft.Windows.SystemHost');
}

// ─── IPv4-First for Chromium renderer (fixes Gemini fetch() on IPv6 networks) ─
// Appended BEFORE app.whenReady() — Chromium reads CLI switches at launch time.
// This ensures fetch() calls to generativelanguage.googleapis.com use IPv4.
app.commandLine.appendSwitch('disable-ipv6');

let mainWindow;
let isOverlayVisible = true;

// Store active AI request so we can abort it
let activeAIRequest = null;
let activeRequestId = null;

function createWindow() {
  const { width: screenW, height: screenH } = screen.getPrimaryDisplay().workAreaSize;

  mainWindow = new BrowserWindow({
    width: 850,
    height: 720,
    x: screenW - 870,
    y: 20,
    transparent: true,
    frame: false,
    alwaysOnTop: true,
    skipTaskbar: true,
    hasShadow: false,
    focusable: true,
    resizable: true,
    movable: true,
    type: 'toolbar',
    icon: path.join(__dirname, 'assets', 'coco_logo.ico'),
    webPreferences: {
      preload: path.join(__dirname, 'preload.js'),
      contextIsolation: true,
      nodeIntegration: false,
      backgroundThrottling: false,
    }
  });

  // ─── Stealth: Content Protection BEFORE loading content ──────
  mainWindow.setContentProtection(true);
  mainWindow.setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces(true, { visibleOnFullScreen: true });

  mainWindow.loadFile('index.html');

  // Re-apply protection after page loads
  mainWindow.webContents.on('did-finish-load', () => {
    mainWindow.setContentProtection(true);
  });

  mainWindow.on('focus', () => {
    mainWindow.setContentProtection(true);
  });

  mainWindow.on('show', () => {
    mainWindow.setContentProtection(true);
  });

  mainWindow.on('closed', () => {
    mainWindow = null;
  });
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  IPC HANDLERS — Window Management
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

ipcMain.on('set-ignore-mouse-events', (event, ignore, options) => {
  const win = BrowserWindow.fromWebContents(event.sender);
  if (win) win.setIgnoreMouseEvents(ignore, options);
});

ipcMain.on('minimize-app', () => {
  if (mainWindow) mainWindow.minimize();
});

ipcMain.on('toggle-maximize-app', () => {
  if (mainWindow) {
    if (mainWindow.isMaximized()) {
      mainWindow.unmaximize();
    } else {
      mainWindow.maximize();
    }
  }
});

ipcMain.on('close-app', () => {
  app.quit();
});

ipcMain.on('toggle-window', () => {
  toggleOverlay();
});

ipcMain.on('set-opacity', (event, opacity) => {
  if (mainWindow) mainWindow.setOpacity(opacity);
});

ipcMain.on('move-to-edge', (event, edge) => {
  if (!mainWindow) return;
  const { width: screenW, height: screenH } = screen.getPrimaryDisplay().workAreaSize;
  const [winW, winH] = mainWindow.getSize();
  switch (edge) {
    case 'left':
      mainWindow.setPosition(20, Math.floor((screenH - winH) / 2));
      break;
    case 'right':
      mainWindow.setPosition(screenW - winW - 20, Math.floor((screenH - winH) / 2));
      break;
    case 'center':
      mainWindow.center();
      break;
  }
});

// ─── Stealth Mode: Window Resize ──────────────────────────────
ipcMain.on('set-window-size', (event, { width, height }) => {
  if (!mainWindow) return;
  const bounds = mainWindow.getBounds();
  const { width: screenW } = screen.getPrimaryDisplay().workAreaSize;
  // Re-anchor to whichever edge is closer
  const centerX = bounds.x + bounds.width / 2;
  let newX;
  if (centerX > screenW / 2) {
    // Right-anchored: keep right edge in place
    newX = bounds.x + bounds.width - width;
  } else {
    // Left-anchored: keep left edge in place
    newX = bounds.x;
  }
  mainWindow.setBounds({
    x: Math.max(0, newX),
    y: bounds.y,
    width,
    height: height || bounds.height
  });
});

// ─── Stealth Mode: Click-Through (Ghost) ──────────────────────
ipcMain.on('set-clickthrough', (event, enabled) => {
  if (!mainWindow) return;
  if (enabled) {
    mainWindow.setIgnoreMouseEvents(true, { forward: true });
  } else {
    mainWindow.setIgnoreMouseEvents(false);
  }
});

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  IPC HANDLERS — API Keys
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

ipcMain.handle('get-api-keys', () => {
  return {
    cerebras: process.env.CEREBRAS_API_KEY || '',
    deepgram: process.env.DEEPGRAM_API_KEY || '',
    gemini:   process.env.GEMINI_API_KEY   || '',
    nvidia:   process.env.BUILD_NVIDIA_API_KEY || '',
    groq:     process.env.GROQ_API_KEY     || '',
  };
});

ipcMain.on('open-external', (event, url) => {
  if (url && typeof url === 'string') {
    shell.openExternal(url);
  }
});

ipcMain.handle('capture-screen', async () => {
  const primaryDisplay = screen.getPrimaryDisplay();
  const scale = primaryDisplay.scaleFactor || 1;
  const width = Math.round(primaryDisplay.bounds.width * scale);
  const height = Math.round(primaryDisplay.bounds.height * scale);

  const sources = await desktopCapturer.getSources({
    types: ['screen'],
    thumbnailSize: { width, height } // Native 1:1 screen resolution — ultra-sharp text OCR for vision AI
  });

  if (sources.length === 0) throw new Error('No screen sources found');

  // On multi-monitor setups, match the source to the primary display
  const primarySource =
    sources.find(s => String(s.display_id) === String(primaryDisplay.id)) ||
    sources[0];

  return primarySource.thumbnail.toDataURL('image/png');
});

ipcMain.handle('get-system-audio-source-id', async () => {
  const sources = await desktopCapturer.getSources({
    types: ['screen']
  });
  
  if (sources.length > 0) {
    return sources[0].id;
  }
  throw new Error('No audio loopback screen source found');
});

ipcMain.handle('get-cerebras-models', async () => {
  if (!cerebras) return [];
  const apiKey = process.env.CEREBRAS_API_KEY;
  if (!apiKey) return [];
  return await cerebras.getModels(apiKey);
});


// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  IPC HANDLERS — Cerebras AI (Streaming)
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

ipcMain.on('ai-stream-request', (event, { question, model, context, requestId }) => {
  const cerebrasKey = process.env.CEREBRAS_API_KEY;
  const groqKey     = process.env.GROQ_API_KEY;

  // ── Helper: try Groq as silent fallback ──────────────────────
  function tryGroqFallback(reason) {
    if (!groq || !groqKey) {
      console.warn(`[AI] Groq fallback unavailable (${reason}). No fallback engine.`);
      if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
        event.sender.send('ai-stream-error', { requestId, error: `Primary AI failed: ${reason}` });
      }
      return;
    }
    console.warn(`[AI] Cerebras failed (${reason}) — switching to Groq fallback silently...`);
    if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
      // Notify UI with a subtle warning (not a full error)
      event.sender.send('ai-stream-fallback', { requestId, engine: 'groq', reason });
    }
    activeRequestId = requestId;

    // MID-STREAM WATCHDOG for Groq fallback — same 15s rolling timeout
    let groqMidStreamTimer = null;
    const resetGroqWatchdog = () => {
      clearTimeout(groqMidStreamTimer);
      groqMidStreamTimer = setTimeout(() => {
        if (activeAIRequest) {
          console.warn('[AI] Groq mid-stream stall detected (>15s no new chunk) — aborting.');
          try { activeAIRequest.abort(); } catch (_) {}
          activeAIRequest = null;
          activeRequestId = null;
          if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
            event.sender.send('ai-stream-error', { requestId, error: 'AI stream timed out (Groq stalled for 15s). Please try again.' });
          }
        }
      }, 15000);
    };
    // Start watchdog immediately — Groq should send first chunk within 15s
    resetGroqWatchdog();

    activeAIRequest = groq.streamCompletion(groqKey, question, {
      model: groq.DEFAULT_MODEL,
      context: context || {},
      onChunk: (chunk, fullText) => {
        resetGroqWatchdog(); // Reset rolling watchdog on every Groq chunk
        if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
          event.sender.send('ai-stream-chunk', { requestId, chunk, fullText });
        }
      },
      onDone: (fullText) => {
        clearTimeout(groqMidStreamTimer);
        activeAIRequest = null;
        activeRequestId = null;
        if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
          event.sender.send('ai-stream-done', { requestId, fullText });
        }
      },
      onError: (err) => {
        clearTimeout(groqMidStreamTimer);
        activeAIRequest = null;
        activeRequestId = null;
        if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
          event.sender.send('ai-stream-error', { requestId, error: `Both Cerebras and Groq failed: ${err.message}` });
        }
      },
    });
  }

  // ── No Cerebras? Go straight to Groq ─────────────────────────
  if (!cerebras || !cerebrasKey) {
    tryGroqFallback('Cerebras not configured');
    return;
  }

  // Only abort previous request if it has NOT started streaming yet (stuck in queue)
  if (activeAIRequest && !activeAIRequest.hasStreamedTokens) {
    if (activeRequestId && !event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
      event.sender.send('ai-stream-aborted', { requestId: activeRequestId });
    }
    try { activeAIRequest.abort(); } catch (_) {}
    activeAIRequest = null;
    activeRequestId = null;
  }

  // ── Primary: Cerebras — fallback to Groq on error or >1000ms delay ────────────
  activeRequestId = requestId;
  let receivedFirstChunk = false;

  // WATCHDOG: Fires if first chunk takes >1000ms (Cerebras latency spike → failover)
  let firstChunkTimer = setTimeout(() => {
    if (!receivedFirstChunk && activeAIRequest) {
      console.warn('[AI] Cerebras first chunk delayed >1000ms — failing over to Groq LPUs...');
      if (activeAIRequest.abort) activeAIRequest.abort();
      activeAIRequest = null;
      activeRequestId = null;
      tryGroqFallback('Cerebras response latency > 1000ms');
    }
  }, 1000);

  // MID-STREAM WATCHDOG: Fires if stream stalls silently for >15s after first chunk.
  // Prevents the UI spinner from hanging forever if Cerebras drops the connection mid-generation.
  let midStreamTimer = null;
  const resetMidStreamWatchdog = () => {
    clearTimeout(midStreamTimer);
    midStreamTimer = setTimeout(() => {
      if (activeAIRequest) {
        console.warn('[AI] Cerebras mid-stream stall detected (>15s no new chunk) — aborting.');
        try { activeAIRequest.abort(); } catch (_) {}
        activeAIRequest = null;
        activeRequestId = null;
        if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
          event.sender.send('ai-stream-error', { requestId, error: 'AI stream timed out (stalled for 15s mid-response). Please try again.' });
        }
      }
    }, 15000);
  };

  const reqObj = cerebras.streamCompletion(cerebrasKey, question, {
    model: model || cerebras.DEFAULT_MODEL,
    context: context || {},
    onChunk: (chunk, fullText) => {
      receivedFirstChunk = true;
      if (reqObj) reqObj.hasStreamedTokens = true;
      clearTimeout(firstChunkTimer);
      resetMidStreamWatchdog(); // Reset rolling 15s watchdog on every chunk
      if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
        event.sender.send('ai-stream-chunk', { requestId, chunk, fullText });
      }
    },
    onDone: (fullText) => {
      clearTimeout(firstChunkTimer);
      clearTimeout(midStreamTimer);
      activeAIRequest = null;
      activeRequestId = null;
      if (!event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
        event.sender.send('ai-stream-done', { requestId, fullText });
      }
    },
    onError: (err) => {
      clearTimeout(firstChunkTimer);
      clearTimeout(midStreamTimer);
      activeAIRequest = null;
      activeRequestId = null;
      // ⚡ Auto-fallback to Groq instead of showing error
      tryGroqFallback(err.message || 'unknown error');
    },
  });
  activeAIRequest = reqObj;
});

ipcMain.on('ai-stream-abort', (event) => {
  if (activeAIRequest) {
    if (activeRequestId && event.sender && !event.sender.isDestroyed()) {
      event.sender.send('ai-stream-aborted', { requestId: activeRequestId });
    }
    activeAIRequest.abort();
    activeAIRequest = null;
    activeRequestId = null;
  }
});

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  Window Toggle & Panic
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

function toggleOverlay() {
  if (!mainWindow) return;
  isOverlayVisible = !isOverlayVisible;
  if (isOverlayVisible) {
    mainWindow.show();
    mainWindow.setContentProtection(true);
    mainWindow.focus();
  } else {
    mainWindow.hide();
  }
}

function panicHide() {
  if (!mainWindow) return;
  mainWindow.hide();
  isOverlayVisible = false;
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  App Lifecycle
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

app.whenReady().then(() => {
  createWindow();

  // ─── Auto-Update: Check for updates silently ───────────────
  if (autoUpdater && app.isPackaged) {
    autoUpdater.on('update-available', (info) => {
      console.log(`🆕 Update available: v${info.version}`);
      if (mainWindow && mainWindow.webContents) {
        mainWindow.webContents.send('update-available', info.version);
      }
    });

    autoUpdater.on('update-downloaded', (info) => {
      console.log(`✅ Update downloaded: v${info.version} — will install on restart`);
      if (mainWindow && mainWindow.webContents) {
        mainWindow.webContents.send('update-downloaded', info.version);
      }
    });

    autoUpdater.on('error', (err) => {
      console.warn('⚠️ Auto-update error:', err.message);
    });

    // Check for updates 3 seconds after launch (non-blocking)
    setTimeout(() => {
      autoUpdater.checkForUpdates().catch(err => {
        console.warn('⚠️ Update check failed:', err.message);
      });
    }, 3000);

    // Re-check every 30 minutes while app is running
    setInterval(() => {
      autoUpdater.checkForUpdates().catch(() => {});
    }, 30 * 60 * 1000);
  }

  // ─── IPC: Force install update now ─────────────────────────
  ipcMain.on('install-update-now', () => {
    if (autoUpdater) {
      autoUpdater.quitAndInstall(false, true);
    }
  });

  // Global Hotkeys
  globalShortcut.register('CommandOrControl+Shift+H', toggleOverlay);
  globalShortcut.register('CommandOrControl+Shift+P', panicHide);

  // Ctrl+Shift+Q = Emergency instant quit / terminate app completely
  globalShortcut.register('CommandOrControl+Shift+Q', () => {
    console.log('[System] Emergency Quit shortcut triggered (Ctrl+Shift+Q)');
    app.quit();
  });

  // Ctrl+Shift+A = Fresh single-shot analyze (always clears buffer)
  globalShortcut.register('CommandOrControl+Shift+A', () => {
    if (mainWindow && mainWindow.webContents) {
      mainWindow.webContents.send('analyze-screen-fresh');
    }
  });

  // Ctrl+Shift+S = Add screenshot to current problem (multi-screenshot mode)
  globalShortcut.register('CommandOrControl+Shift+S', () => {
    if (mainWindow && mainWindow.webContents) {
      mainWindow.webContents.send('analyze-screen-add');
    }
  });

  globalShortcut.register('CommandOrControl+Shift+G', () => {
    if (mainWindow && mainWindow.webContents) {
      mainWindow.webContents.send('cycle-stealth');
    }
  });

  globalShortcut.register('Alt+Left', () => {
    if (mainWindow) {
      const bounds = mainWindow.getBounds();
      const { width: screenW } = screen.getPrimaryDisplay().workAreaSize;
      if (bounds.x > screenW / 2) mainWindow.setPosition(20, bounds.y);
    }
  });

  globalShortcut.register('Alt+Right', () => {
    if (mainWindow) {
      const bounds = mainWindow.getBounds();
      const { width: screenW } = screen.getPrimaryDisplay().workAreaSize;
      const [winW] = mainWindow.getSize();
      if (bounds.x < screenW / 2) mainWindow.setPosition(screenW - winW - 20, bounds.y);
    }
  });

  app.on('activate', () => {
    if (BrowserWindow.getAllWindows().length === 0) createWindow();
  });
});

app.on('will-quit', () => {
  globalShortcut.unregisterAll();
});

app.on('window-all-closed', () => {
  if (process.platform !== 'darwin') app.quit();
});

const { contextBridge, ipcRenderer } = require('electron');

  onUpdateDownloaded: (callback) => {
    ipcRenderer.on('update-downloaded', (event, version) => callback(version));
  },
  installUpdateNow: () => ipcRenderer.send('install-update-now'),

  // ─── Auth Sync ────────────────────────────────────────────────

  },

  // ─── Identify ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
  isElectron: true,
});

Section D: Cerebras LPU Streaming Driver SEC D

services/cerebras.js connects directly to Cerebras Wafer-Scale Engine clusters, achieving sub-200ms TTFT streaming completions with automatic 402/429 failover triggers.

services/cerebras.js Primary Inference Driver · 279 lines 
/* ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   CocoAI — Cerebras AI Service
   Streaming chat completions via Cerebras (OpenAI-compatible API)
   ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ */

const https = require('https');

const CEREBRAS_BASE = 'api.cerebras.ai';

// Available models on Cerebras (updated September 2026)
const MODELS = {
  'gpt-120b':  'gpt-oss-120b',
  'qwen-27b':  'qwen-3.8-27b',
  'gemma-31b': 'gemma-4-31b',
};

const DEFAULT_MODEL = 'gpt-oss-120b';

/**
 * Build the system prompt for interview context
 */
function buildSystemPrompt(context = {}) {
  let prompt = `You are CocoAI, an elite AI interview copilot assisting a candidate in a live technical interview. Your answers MUST sound like a smart, natural human candidate speaking — NOT an AI textbook.

CRITICAL ANSWER QUALITY & LENGTH RULES:
1. STRICT LENGTH PROPORTIONALITY:
   - Simple/Quick Questions (e.g., definitions, concepts, quick comparisons): 40 to 75 words MAX. Give a 2-3 sentence punchy answer.
   - Medium/Technical Questions (e.g., system design, architecture, framework concepts): 80 to 140 words MAX. Clear bullet points.
   - Coding Problems: Output ONLY the clean runnable code block + 2 lines explaining approach, O(Time), and O(Space).
   - Behavioral Questions: Use STAR method in 100 to 140 words total (1 short bullet each for Situation, Task, Action, Result).

2. ZERO FLUFF / NO "AI COOKED" PREAMBLES:
   - NEVER start with pleasantries or preambles like "Sure!", "Certainly!", "Great question!", "Here is a breakdown...", "In technical interviews...".
   - Start IMMEDIATELY with the answer on line 1.
   - NEVER end with conclusions like "In summary", "To conclude", "Hope this helps!".

3. NATURAL HUMAN SPEAKING TONE:
   - Use direct, spoken English that the candidate can read aloud effortlessly.
   - Avoid overly formal academic jargon or textbook definitions. Use practical industry terms.

4. ROBUSTNESS TO TRANSCRIPTION & PRONUNCIATION ERRORS:
   - The question transcript you receive is captured in real-time from a speech-to-text engine. It may contain minor phonetic typos, misheard words, or minor grammar/pronunciation errors (e.g. "reactive native" instead of "React Native", "usestate hook" instead of "useState hook", "docker container" instead of "Docker container").
   - Intelligently reconstruct the interviewer's original intent based on technical context and answer the correct underlying question. Do NOT mention or point out any transcription errors.

5. STRICT ANSWER LENGTH COMPLIANCE:
   - Enforce these word counts strictly. Never exceed them under any circumstances. Keep your answers concise, practical, and conversational.`;

  if (context.resume) {
    // Sanitize and cap resume to max 1,500 words (~6000 chars) to prevent token context window bloat
    const cleanResume = String(context.resume).trim();
    const words = cleanResume.split(/\s+/);
    const cappedResume = words.length > 1500 ? words.slice(0, 1500).join(' ') + ' ...[truncated]' : cleanResume;

    prompt += `\n\nCANDIDATE'S RESUME (PRIMARY SOURCE FOR CANDIDATE'S PERSONALITY, SKILLS & PROJECTS):\n${cappedResume}\n\nIMPORTANT CONTEXT INSTRUCTION FOR PERSONAL & BEHAVIORAL QUESTIONS:
The resume above defines the candidate's professional identity, technical skills, real-world projects, work history, education, and domain expertise.
For ANY personal, behavioral, or experience-based questions (e.g., "Tell me about yourself", "What are your strengths?", "Describe a challenging project you built", "What technologies do you prefer?", "Why should we hire you?"):
1. Speak in FIRST PERSON ("I", "my", "we") as the candidate.
2. Ground your answer directly in the candidate's actual projects, programming languages, frameworks, and job experience listed in the resume.
3. Reflect the candidate's professional background and technical persona.
4. Keep spoken personal responses concise (60-90 words max), natural, and confident.`;
  }
  if (context.jobDescription) {
    const cleanJD = String(context.jobDescription).trim();
    const jdWords = cleanJD.split(/\s+/);
    const cappedJD = jdWords.length > 1000 ? jdWords.slice(0, 1000).join(' ') + ' ...[truncated]' : cleanJD;
    prompt += `\n\nJOB DESCRIPTION:\n${cappedJD}`;
  }
  if (context.transcript && context.transcript.length > 0) {
    const recentTranscript = context.transcript.slice(-10).map(t =>
      `${t.role}: ${t.text}`
    ).join('\n');
    prompt += `\n\nRECENT INTERVIEW TRANSCRIPT:\n${recentTranscript}`;
  }

  return prompt;
}

/**
 * Stream a chat completion from Cerebras API
 * @param {string} apiKey - Cerebras API key
 * @param {string} question - The interview question
 * @param {object} options - { model, context, onChunk, onDone, onError }
 * @returns {object} - { abort() } to cancel the request
 */
function streamCompletion(apiKey, question, options = {}) {
  const {
    model = DEFAULT_MODEL,
    context = {},
    onChunk = () => {},
    onDone = () => {},
    onError = () => {},
    attempt = 0,
    maxRetries = 3,
  } = options;

  let isAborted = false;
  let activeReq = null;

  const systemPrompt = buildSystemPrompt(context);

  const payload = JSON.stringify({
    model: model,
    messages: [
      { role: 'system', content: systemPrompt },
      { role: 'user', content: question }
    ],
    stream: true,
    max_tokens: 1024,
    temperature: 0.7,
    top_p: 0.9,
  });

  activeReq = https.request({
    hostname: CEREBRAS_BASE,
    path: '/v1/chat/completions',
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'Authorization': `Bearer ${apiKey}`,
      'Accept': 'text/event-stream',
    },
  }, (res) => {
    // ── Handle 429 Rate Limit & 402 Payment Required — Instant Failover to Groq LPUs ──
    if (res.statusCode === 429 || res.statusCode === 402) {
      if (!isAborted) {
        const reason = res.statusCode === 402 ? 'Cerebras quota exhausted (402)' : `Cerebras 429 Rate Limit Exceeded on ${model}`;
        console.warn(`⚡ ${reason} — instant failover to Groq LPUs...`);
        onError(new Error(reason));
      }
      return;
    }

    if (res.statusCode !== 200) {
      let errorBody = '';
      res.on('data', (chunk) => { errorBody += chunk.toString(); });
      res.on('end', () => {
        if (!isAborted) {
          onError(new Error(`Cerebras API error ${res.statusCode}: ${errorBody}`));
        }
      });
      res.on('error', (err) => {
        if (!isAborted) onError(err);
      });
      return;
    }

    let buffer = '';
    let fullText = '';

    res.on('data', (chunk) => {
      if (isAborted) return;
      buffer += chunk.toString();
      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
      buffer = lines.pop() || '';

      for (const line of lines) {
        const trimmed = line.trim();
        if (!trimmed || !trimmed.startsWith('data: ')) continue;

        const data = trimmed.slice(6);
        if (data === '[DONE]') {
          if (!isAborted) onDone(fullText);
          return;
        }

        try {
          const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
          const delta = parsed.choices?.[0]?.delta?.content;
          if (delta && !isAborted) {
            fullText += delta;
            onChunk(delta, fullText);
          }
        } catch (e) {}
      }
    });

    res.on('end', () => {
      if (!isAborted && fullText) {
        onDone(fullText);
      }
    });

    res.on('error', (err) => {
      if (!isAborted) onError(err);
    });
  });

  activeReq.on('error', (err) => {
    if (attempt < maxRetries && !isAborted) {
      const delay = 600 * Math.pow(2, attempt);
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!isAborted) {
          streamCompletion(apiKey, question, {
            ...options,
            attempt: attempt + 1
          });
        }
      }, delay);
      return;
    }
    if (!isAborted) onError(err);
  });

  activeReq.write(payload);
  activeReq.end();

  return {
    abort: () => {
      isAborted = true;
      if (activeReq) {
        try { activeReq.destroy(); } catch (e) {}
      }
    }
  };
}

/**
 * Non-streaming completion (for quick one-shot queries)
 */
function getCompletion(apiKey, question, model = DEFAULT_MODEL) {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    let result = '';
    streamCompletion(apiKey, question, {
      model,
      onChunk: (chunk) => { result += chunk; },
      onDone: (text) => resolve(text),
      onError: reject,
    });
  });
}

/**
 * Fetch available models from Cerebras
 */
function getModels(apiKey) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    const req = https.get({
      hostname: CEREBRAS_BASE,
      path: '/v1/models',
      headers: {
        'Authorization': `Bearer ${apiKey}`,
      },
      timeout: 3000,
    }, (res) => {
      if (res.statusCode !== 200) {
        resolve([]);
        return;
      }
      let body = '';
      res.on('data', chunk => body += chunk);
      res.on('end', () => {
        try {
          const parsed = JSON.parse(body);
          const models = (parsed.data || []).map(m => m.id);
          resolve(models);
        } catch (e) {
          resolve([]);
        }
      });
    });

    req.on('error', () => resolve([]));
    req.on('timeout', () => {
      try { req.destroy(); } catch(e) {}
      resolve([]);
    });
  });
}

module.exports = {
  MODELS,
  DEFAULT_MODEL,
  streamCompletion,
  getCompletion,
  getModels,
  buildSystemPrompt,
};

Section E: Groq Neural Fallback Driver SEC E

services/groq.js provides seamless, zero-latency backup inference when the primary Cerebras provider exhausts rate limits or encounters transient network hiccups.

The resume above defines the candidate's professional identity, technical skills, real-world projects, work history, education, and domain expertise.
